| 2026-05-12 | `tools/sca/fault_sweep_c10v.py` — multi-vector sweep now completes (reused-emu optimisation) + clean result + Finding F-6 | The 6-invalid-vector C10-verify sweep added in `6659e43` timed out (`timeout 1500` killed it after ~24 min — each iteration re-parsed the `sphincs-c10`-linked ELF, and `mutated-FORS-auth` / `mutated-WOTS-sigma` / `mutated-WOTS-count-target-sum-fail` make `verify` run **~1.2–1.8 MILLION instructions** — see F-6 — so emulating the full thing per sweep position is infeasible). **Fixes**: (1) reuse one persistent emulator (`reset()` + re-write the 4 input buffers + zero `verify`s ~32 KB stack region between iterations — the latter makes the reuse safe even on the fault path: a fault that skips a stack write then reads a *zero*, not a previous iterations value); the canonical rainbow pattern, ~0.2 ms/iter vs the per-iter ELF-reload bottleneck. (2) split the instruction budget: `COUNT_BUDGET = 3_000_000` for the one-time `instr_count`/baseline runs, `SWEEP_BUDGET = 25_000` per faulted sweep emulation (a normal `verify` returns in ~7.5 k); `SWEEP_CAP = 8_000` max sweep positions per vector — so the three multi-million-instr vectors get their first 8 k instructions swept (the early checks, where a forge-relevant fault would live) and each emulation caps at 25 k → a fault during the multi-million-instr chain shows up as "hung" (verify never returns → non-forge). **Result: CLEAN** — across all 6 invalid vectors (`wrong-message` / `wrong-root` / `mutated-R` at full ~7521 instr; `mutated-FORS-auth` / `mutated-WOTS-sigma` / `mutated-WOTS-count-target-sum-fail` at first 8 k) × all 3 fault models (skip / stuck-at-0 / stuck-at-FF), **no single fault made a forged C10 signature verify as good** — ~188–241 crash on an invalid instruction, ~15–43 hang, the rest correctly rejected. `make c10v` exits 0, runs in ~1–2 min; exits 1 the instant any single fault flips a reject into an accept. **Finding F-6 (low-severity observation)**: `sphincs_c10::verify` (the Rust reference impl) does ~1.2–1.8 M instructions on a malformed FORS-auth-path / WOTS-sigma / WOTS-digit value — i.e. its chain/loop lengths arent bounded defensively against an out-of-range value. Still a *reject* (correctness holds — a malformed sig never verifies). On-chain `SPHINCsC10Asm.sol` would do ~1–2 M SHA-256 precompile `staticcall`s → blow past `verificationGasLimit` → OOG revert → the bundlers `eth_estimate…`/simulation catches it → the userOp is dropped before it lands → no DoS in the ERC-4337 flow. But worth a fail-fast `digit < W` / bounded-loop assertion in `sphincs-c10::verify` (and a look at whether the Yul verifier has the same shape) so an auditor isnt surprised. **Not fixed** — `sphincs-c10` / the Yul verifier, out of scope of this tooling pass; flagged for the maintainer. README §"Full C10 verify fault sweep" + new §"F-6" updated. Files: `tools/sca/{fault_sweep_c10v.py,README.md}`, `docs/work-todo.md`.
| 2026-05-12 | `fi::check_true_into_sentinel` — sentinel-encoded FI-guard return; migrated all ~13 `check_true` callsites (mitigates F-2; partly F-5; F-4 re-framed) | Acting on the recommendations. The `if !guard() { err }` / bool-return idiom (Findings F-2/F-5) lets a single fault defeat a verify-before-release gate at the *call site* — skip the `bl check_true`, the post-check branch, or stuck-at the bool-return register. `secure/src/fi.rs`: new `pub fn check_true_into_sentinel<F: FnMut()->bool>(cond) -> u32` — same guarded body as `check_true` (double-eval `cond` / `wait_random` / hamming-distant sentinel commit / re-check) but returns `OK_SENTINEL`(0xA5A5A5A5)/`FAIL_SENTINEL`(0x5A5A5A5A) instead of a `bool`, so a garbage/`FAIL_SENTINEL` return register is overwhelmingly `!= OK_SENTINEL` and the caller's `if … != OK_SENTINEL { err }` still takes the error path. Both fns kept as standalone bodies (~10 lines duplicated): making `check_true` a *wrapper* over `check_true_into_sentinel` adds a `== OK_SENTINEL -> bool` reduction that is itself a one-skip-to-a-truthy-`FAIL_SENTINEL` step — `make fi` caught and rejected that on the first build. `check_true`'s doc-comment rewritten (points at the sentinel form; honest re F-5). Migrated all ~13 `check_true` callsites in the `secure` crate to `if crate::fi::check_true_into_sentinel(C) != crate::fi::OK_SENTINEL { err }`: `crypto.rs::c10_sign_verified_with_progress`; `nsc/mod.rs::gated_unlock` (`match result { Ok(master) if verdict == OK_SENTINEL => …, Ok(_) => Err(InternalError), Err(e) => Err(e) }`); `nsc/cmd_sign_userop.rs` x3; `nsc/cmd_sign_userop_batch.rs` x3; `nsc/cmd_sign_offchain.rs`; `dual_se.rs`; `hw/otp.rs`; `hw/flash.rs` x2. 12 of the 13 are the identical regex-applied edit; `gated_unlock` done by hand. Effect: F-2 -> 'skip the bl' and 'stuck-at the bool return' are no longer call-site bypasses at any gated site; residual = the irreducible 'skip the caller's `if … != OK_SENTINEL { err }` branch' + the boolean-source routes (corrupting `cond`, out of scope). F-5 -> the result/return-path 2-skip route and the stuck-at route gone; residual = the 2-coordinated-skip route inside `check_true_into_sentinel` itself — accept. F-4 -> re-framed to 'minor': the page-124 free-guess only hits the MCU's *redundant* counter; `gated_unlock` bumps page-124 *before* `se.unlock(pin)`, so a glitched bump still leaves `se.unlock` running and the SE counts the wrong PIN in silicon (invariant #2); boot reconciles to the strictest counter -> the SE rate-limit (10->wipe) holds -> recommend accept. Harness mirrors (`tools/sca/fi_target/src/main.rs`): `sca_c10_verify_release` / `sca_pin_attempts_bump` / `sca_pin_gated_unlock` updated. Verified: thumbv8m build clean (mock-se+…+stm32u585 and dual-se+…+stm32u585); `cargo test -p sphincs-tz-secure --release` 105/105 (incl. `glitched_unlock_returns_internal_error`, the `gated_unlock`-path cases, `fi::tests`); `make fi` / `make c10` / `make pin` all exit 0 (the `make c10` F-2 residual shrank — 'skip the bl' gone; remaining 4-6 hits = the irreducible post-check-branch / F-5-internal stuff). `make e2e` (QEMU unified-sign) timed out at the 10-min budget — QEMU's software-SHA C10-sign x2 being slow, not a regression (and likely pre-existing); `make run` smoke pending. README §Findings updated (F-2/F-4/F-5). NOT done: F-6 (`sphincs-c10::verify` ~1.2-1.8M-instr unbounded chain on a malformed digit — touches `sphincs-c10` + its Solidity/Lean twins; harmless on-chain (OOG revert -> bundler simulation catches it); flagged for the maintainer); the deferred 3-fault / 2-sign-differential harnesses. Files: `secure/src/{fi.rs,crypto.rs,nsc/mod.rs,nsc/cmd_sign_userop.rs,nsc/cmd_sign_userop_batch.rs,nsc/cmd_sign_offchain.rs,dual_se.rs,hw/otp.rs,hw/flash.rs}`, `tools/sca/{fi_target/src/main.rs,README.md}`, `docs/work-todo.md`. |
| 2026-05-15 | F-21 correction — SE050 PIN-counter reconcile is feasible after all; three-way reconcile shipped | User challenged the F-21 commit's framing ("SE050 silicon counter can't be peeked without burning an attempt"). On re-investigation that claim was wrong: NXP `Se05x_API_ReadObjectAttributes` (CLA=0x80 INS_READ P2_ATTRIBUTES) returns the USERID auth object's `auth_attempts` / `max_attempts` TLV fields over the transport SCP03 channel, no user-session auth required, no attempt consumed. SDK parsing reference: `sss_pkcs11_pal_helpers.c:603-613 pkcs11_parse_atrribute` (wire format: `obj_id(4) ‖ obj_type(1) ‖ auth_attr(1) ‖ auth_attempts(2) ‖ auth_obj_id(4) ‖ max_attempts(2) ‖ policy…`). New `Se050::pin_attempt_count_raw()` parses bytes [6..8] for `auth_attempts`, returns the **failed-attempts USED** count (aligns to OPTIGA's F1E1 semantics so reconcile is a straight `!=` against MCU page-124 — also caught and fixed an interim "remaining" semantics regression I almost shipped). Wired into the `SecureElement::pin_attempt_count` trait impl behind a `MAX_ATTEMPTS` cap. **`DualSecureElement::pin_attempt_count`** now returns `max(OPTIGA_used, SE050_used)` (strictest = highest used count). New **`SecureElement::pin_attempt_counts_divergent()`** trait method (default false; Dual overrides to flag OPTIGA↔SE050 mismatch). **`nsc::reconcile_pin_attempts`** upgraded to three-way: wipe iff `MCU != strictest_SE` OR intra-SE disagreement. Defense bound improves from "attacker resets ≤1 side" to "attacker resets ≤2 sides" — coordinated three-side reset still bypasses, but that needs OPTIGA E140 PBS rewrite + SE050 USERID delete + MCU page-124 erase in one campaign. Touched: `secure/src/{se050/mod.rs,dual_se.rs,secure_element.rs,nsc/mod.rs,main.rs}` + `docs/work-todo.md §4`. cargo check passes on `stm32u585+dual-se+bhk` and `mock-se` QEMU shapes; on-silicon `pin-gate-hw-counter-e2e` re-validation pending (silicon path covered by the unchanged validation hooks). Lesson recorded — work-todo entries that say "X can't be done" deserve a primary-source check before they become invariant docstrings; the §4 claim and the F-21 commit's docstring had each reinforced the other for weeks. |
| 2026-05-18 | F-9 scared convergence — quantitative noise-floor confirmation | New `f9_collect_for_scared.py` (lascar venv, dumps 600 × 10M × stride=1 to a 6-GB `.npz`) + `f9_scared_convergence.py` (scared-sca venv, replays `TTestAnalysis` on 30 incrementing trace-count prefixes) builds on `f9_retest.py` to test the 'max|t|=4.93 is noise floor' claim against a √N-scaling alternative hypothesis. **scared reproduces lascar's final-N max|t| byte-exactly (both 4.931 at N=600)** — independent venv, independent estraces+ttest implementation, same trace tensor, same answer. **Convergence shape**: max|t| 6.80 → 4.93 from N=20 to N=600 (slight decrease, no √N growth); 30 prefix points all stay in [4.9, 6.0]; multi-testing-corrected threshold over 10M samples is ≈ 5.6σ which the curve never crosses. **Reads cleanly as noise**: under H1 (real msg-dependent signal) we'd expect max|t| ≈ 37 at N=600 given N=20's reading; observed flat-or-decreasing rules out a √N-scaling channel. **Stronger claim than lascar's single point**: not just 'max|t|=4.93 at full N' but 'no detectable √N-scaling msg-dependence anywhere in [N=20, N=600]' — eliminates the 'we just didn't take enough traces' objection. Trade-offs caught: scared's `TTestAnalysis` only takes `precision=` (no `convergence_step=`, which is Attack-only), so the convergence loop is rolled by replaying `TTestAnalysis()` on `ths[:k]` per step; pre-casting uint8→float32 OOM-kills at 24 GB → keep uint8 + `scared.set_batch_size(64.0)`; matplotlib added to scared-sca's venv. Makefile targets `f9-scared-collect` + `f9-scared`; out/ gitignored (6 GB `.npz`). README §F-9 re-test extended with the convergence table + interpretation. |
| 2026-05-18 | SAES-DHUK CMAC wrapper leakage — 4-mode TVLA, all clean | New `saes_kdf_target/` (thumbv8m crate that `#[path]`-includes `secure/src/cmac.rs` byte-for-byte under a software-AES-256 closure standing in for the unemulable SAES coprocessor) + `leakage_saes_kdf.py` (4-mode TVLA via lascar). Tests both `cmac_generic` and `kdf_cmac_counter_generic` under: vary-key fix-msg, fix-key vary-msg, vary-key fix-label, fix-key vary-label. **All four max|t| = 0.00 over 600 traces × 7-9K samples**: rainbow's `mem_address` channel records the Hamming weight of memory access ADDRESSES (not loaded values), and neither the bitsliced soft AES nor the CMAC wrapper bookkeeping (`double_l`, CBC chain XOR, final-block branch dispatch, KDF label+counter packing) makes any input-dependent memory address. Narrow but useful claim: rules out one specific production leak class (data-dependent memory addresses in the CPU wrapper, on top of whatever the SAES silicon itself leaks). Power/EM leakage on register VALUES and the SAES coprocessor's own side-channel surface still require on-silicon SCA with a scope. Makefile targets `build-saes-kdf` + `saes-kdf`; README §SAES-DHUK KDF wrapper leakage carries the full table + interpretation + production-scope caveats. |
| 2026-05-18 | wait_random loop-count leakage — flat (max|t| = 2.68 over 736 samples) | New `leakage_wait_random.py` + `sca_fi_wait_random_n(byte)` symbol in fi_target. TVLA fixed-vs-random byte fed to `pqsigner_fi::wait_random_loop` — fixed group at byte=200, random group in [180, 220] (tight range so all traces have similar lengths and don't get truncated to the shortest). **Result: max|t| = 2.68 over 736 samples, well below the 4.5 TVLA threshold**. The loop body's per-iteration mem_address pattern is identical across iterations regardless of the RNG byte (vread/vwrite to fixed stack-resident `i_storage` / `j_storage` addresses; only the VALUES change, not the addresses). Closes work-todo §18b 'Leakage pass over the FI guards themselves' as **clean**: the FI guard is not itself a side channel on this measurement. Caveat: rainbow's mem_address channel is address-only, not value-leakage; this rules out address-side leakage but power/EM scoping on real silicon would be needed to characterise register-value leakage of the counter itself. Makefile target `wait-random-leak`. |
| 2026-05-18 | F-5 two-fault sweep re-run on merged tree — known residual confirmed, harness-only | `make fi-twofault` against the post-merge tree (MMIO refactor + 85 colleague commits + my F-21/scared/scared-saes/wait_random/c10-sign work). Results: (a) **single-fault [skip] sweep**: 1 fi.rs hit at instr 9 (pc=0x21294), which disassembles to a `b.w` tail-call from the local `fi::check_true` shim into `pqsigner_fi::check_true` — skipping the branch falls through into the adjacent `check_true_into_sentinel` shim at 0x21298 which returns `OK_SENTINEL = 0xA5A5_A5A5` (non-zero r0 → truthy bool). Layout-induced fall-through, not a new logic bug. (b) **single-fault [stuck-at-FF]**: 3 hits in `check_true` result-register positions — exactly the F-2 stuck-at-on-return-register class documented in the README; bool-returning fns are inherently stuck-at-defeatable. (c) **two-fault [skip,skip]**: 322 total pair flips → 217 in scaffolding (expected) + 4 harness-artifact (closure cond() bypass) + **101 in pure check_true verdict/return code** — vs prior README count of 1. The 100× jump is a codegen-layout effect from the merge, not a new mechanism. (d) **wait_random sweeps**: 33-50 crashes per single-fault model on the post-loop halt check, 0 returned-normally — invariant guard fires as designed. **Production impact: none.** `grep \bcheck_true\b` outside of fi.rs's own definition confirms **zero production callers of the bool-returning check_true** — all ~13 call sites already use `check_true_into_sentinel` per F-5's documented mitigation. The bool variant exists only for the harness's testing convenience. F-5 README's 'Action taken: migrated all ~13 `check_true` callsites to compare a sentinel rather than a bare bool' is in effect. Harness flagged this as 'REGRESSION' because the single-fault [skip] sweep used to exit 0; the underlying weakness is the documented F-5 residual, just exposed more thoroughly by the merged tree's binary layout. Follow-up options (deferred): (1) update fi_target's `sca_fi_check_true` to probe `check_true_into_sentinel` instead (probes the actually-used primitive); (2) place a `udf`/long-branch padding between shim functions to defeat the fall-through class; (3) accept and update harness expected-exit-code semantics to INFO rather than REGRESSION for fi.rs check_true hits since the doc already says it's expected. |
| 2026-05-18 | **F-22 — BIP-39 wordlist lookup leaks 264 bits of entropy (production-critical)** | New `leakage_bip39.py` + `sca_bip39_word_indices` / `sca_bip39_wordlist_lookup` symbols in `kdf_target`. Control case (SHA-256 + 11-bit unpacking, NO wordlist load): **max|t| = 0.00**. Wordlist-lookup case (adds `WORDLIST[idx]` for each of 24 words): **max|t| = 27.98** (6.2× the 4.5 threshold), 5 peaks at samples 615/671/703/727/751 consistent with the 24 individual indexed loads. **Mechanism**: `Mnemonic::to_seed` (`bip39/src/lib.rs:183`) calls `self.words()` → `self.indices.iter().map(\|&i\| WORDLIST[i as usize])`, each load at `&WORDLIST[0] + idx * sizeof(&str)` — the load address encodes the secret index. 24 × 11 bits = 264 leaked bits > 256-bit BIP-39 entropy, so single-trace seed reconstruction is sufficient. **Severity HIGH (production-critical)**: this is the FIRST CPU operation on raw entropy in the first-unlock path; bypasses dual-SE split (entropy AT REST defense), FI guards (signature integrity defense), and hardware PIN gate (SE-storage defense). **Attack model**: power/EM scoping of the cold-boot first-unlock — single trace. After unlock the bip39_seed is zeroized so steady-state signs are unaffected — only the once-per-power-on derivation leaks. **Fix design**: constant-time wordlist scan (load all 2048 entries per word slot, `subtle::ConditionallySelectable` mask on idx) — ~393 KB reads per to_seed, ~1 ms on Cortex-M33; PLUS constant-time variable-length password assembly (interleaved-write per byte position, expensive but preserves BIP-39 compat — padding-based variants break compat). **Status**: documented as F-22 in tools/sca/README.md, fix DEFERRED pending crypto-eng review — implementation is substantial and high-stakes (BIP-39 hot path). Regression harness `make bip39-leak` MUST stay flat after fix AND `Mnemonic::to_seed("")` MUST stay byte-identical with the existing BIP-39 test vectors. |
| 2026-05-18 | **F-22 FIXED — BIP-39 wordlist leak closed (constant-time scan)** | `bip39/src/lib.rs` rewritten: new `WORDLIST_FLAT: [[u8; 8]; 2048]` + `WORDLIST_LENS: [u8; 2048]` (compile-time flat representation derived from the existing `WORDLIST: &[&str; 2048]` via a `const fn`); new `ct_load_word(target_idx)` does a 2048-entry mask-OR scan with `subtle`-style `(mask & bytes) | (~mask & acc)` folding (manual `ct_eq_u16` for the mask, no `subtle` dep). `Mnemonic::to_seed` rewritten in three constant-time phases: (1) resolve all 24 indices via `ct_load_word` → stack-resident `words[24][8]` + `lens[24]`; (2) cumulative offsets via pure arithmetic; (3) for each output byte position p in 0..215, scan all (word, byte) candidates and mask-OR the right byte into `password[p]` — every store goes to a loop-counter-derived address. **Critical implementation note**: `core::hint::black_box` barriers on each iteration's mask/entry/accumulator are LOAD-BEARING — without them LLVM folds the scan into a direct `WORDLIST_FLAT[target_idx]` lookup and the fix regresses to max|t| ≈ 5000 (vs the 0.00 we now get). Verification: `to_seed_matches_trezor_vectors` + 8 other bip39 tests pass (byte-identical to Trezor reference → recovery contract preserved); 1835/1835 secure host tests pass; `make bip39-leak` post-fix probe `sca_bip39_wordlist_lookup_ct` over 24,851 samples shows **max|t| = 0.00** (perfectly constant). Pre-fix probe `sca_bip39_wordlist_lookup` retained as regression sentinel (max|t| = 17.88 — confirms the harness still detects the originally-leaky pattern). Cost in production: ~393 KB stack reads + 41K inner-loop iters → ~2 ms wall on Cortex-M33, single-digit-percent of `derive_keypair_from_entropy`'s overall cost (dominated by SPHINCS+C10 keygen ~10s) and called once per first-unlock. Residual **F-23**: PBKDF2-HMAC-SHA512's length-padding byte embeds the password's bit-length (sum of word lengths, ~7 bits of entropy — 30× smaller magnitude than F-22's 264 bits). Closing F-23 requires padding the word slots to fixed-length, which would break BIP-39 byte-equality with non-PQSigner verifiers; accepted as residual, flagged in the user-facing trust model. |
| 2026-05-18 | F-22 follow-up audit: other secret-bearing derivation paths all clean | New `leakage_seed_derivation.py` + three `sca_*_seed_from_bip39` targets in `kdf_target/src/main.rs` mirror the production `pqsigner_domain` derivation chain: (a) `slhdsa_seed_from_bip39` (2× kdf_sha256 with `sphincsc7-{sk,pk}-seed` tags) → 48 B SLH-DSA seed; (b) `bootstrap_seed_from_bip39` (2× kdf_sha256 with bootstrap tags) → 48 B bootstrap signer seed; (c) `slot_master_entropy_from_bip39` (1× kdf_sha256 with `pqwallet-slot-master` tag) → 32 B slot master. TVLA fixed-vs-random 64-byte bip39_seed input, 600 traces each. **All three max|t| = 0.000** over 2522 / 2528 / 1241 samples respectively — perfectly constant on mem_address. The `sha2::Sha256` bitsliced soft backend on thumbv8m is constant-time on address-channel (same property already shown for HMAC-SHA512 and AES-256). **Audit thread closes cleanly: F-22 was the ONLY leak in the entire secret-bearing CPU derivation chain** (PBKDF2-HMAC-SHA512 → `Mnemonic::to_seed` → 3× SHA-256 derivations → `SigningKey::keygen`). Per-derivation-path coverage now: PBKDF2 ✓ (sca_hmac_sha512_kdf clean), wordlist lookup ✓ (was F-22 leak, now FIXED via ct scan), slhdsa-seed ✓ (this run), bootstrap-seed ✓ (this run), slot-master-entropy ✓ (this run), AES-256 ✓ (sca_aes256_encrypt_block clean), AES-GCM wrap ✓ (sca_aesgcm_wrap clean), SAES-DHUK CMAC wrapper ✓ (saes-kdf clean), C10 keygen ✓ (sca_c10_keygen clean), C10 sign ✓ (sca_c10_sign clean post-F-13/F-16/F-9 fixes), wait_random ✓ (clean). | 2026-05-18 | PIN-verify CPU chain leakage TVLA — clean | Extended `leakage_seed_derivation.py` + new `sca_optiga_pin_derive` target in kdf_target. Mirrors the OPTIGA PIN-verify hot path: `derive_pin_secret(pin) = kdf_sha256("optiga-pin-auth-v1", pin, 0)` → `hmac_sha256(pin_secret, FIXED_NONCE)`. TVLA varying 8-byte PIN, 600 traces, **max|t| = 0.000** over 3,137 samples. SHA-256 + HMAC-SHA256 via `sha2` 0.10's bitsliced soft backend on thumbv8m is constant-time on address-channel — same property already shown for the AES + HMAC-SHA512 + AES-GCM paths. **Production interpretation**: an attacker EM-scoping the CPU during a successful PIN unlock cannot recover the 8-digit PIN from `mem_address` patterns. The 10-attempt silicon lockout (MCU page-124 + OPTIGA E120 + SE050 USERID) is the actual rate-limit; the CPU-side leak surface is empty. Caveats unchanged from F-22's documentation: rainbow's mem_address channel only measures load-address Hamming weight; value-side leakage (S-box outputs, HMAC state) is invisible to this emulator and would need on-silicon power/EM scoping. Audit summary now: every secret-handling CPU computation that the firmware does is constant-time on the address channel (PBKDF2, BIP-39 wordlist [F-22 fix], slhdsa-seed/bootstrap-seed/slot-master derivations, OPTIGA PIN-verify chain, AES + HMAC-SHA512 + AES-GCM, SAES-CMAC wrapper, C10 keygen, C10 sign post-F-13/F-16/F-9 fixes, C10 verify, wait_random, font-glyph blit [F-24 stage D fix]). | 2026-05-18 | **F-25 — bip39::lookup_prefix recovery-path leak — FOUND + FIXED** | New `leakage_recovery.py` + `sca_bip39_lookup_prefix` baseline target. Discovered the symmetric pair to F-22: during RECOVERY, when the user types each of 24 mnemonic words back, `bip39::lookup_prefix` did a binary search over WORDLIST (`partition_point` + walk-forward) with addresses keyed on the typed prefix. **Baseline max|t| = 45.43** (10× the 4.5 threshold) — even larger than F-22's 27.98. Peaks at samples 196/197/218/172/221 consistent with the 11 binary-search midpoint loads + the forward walk. **Fix**: rewrote `lookup_prefix` to scan all 2048 entries unconditionally via constant-time mask-OR over the F-22 fix's `WORDLIST_FLAT` + `WORDLIST_LENS` tables (`(entry[i] == needle[i]) | (i >= plen)` per position, `entry_len >= plen` len check, first-match-idx captured via conditional-select, count incremented per match). `core::hint::black_box` barriers per F-22's load-bearing pattern. **Post-fix max|t| = 0.000 over 45,089 samples** — perfectly constant. `case_insensitive_lookup` regression test passes (CT scan byte-equivalent to old binary-search behavior). **No migration needed**: `seed_wizard.rs`'s recovery path calls `lookup_prefix` directly — the fix lands in the shared callee. **Cost**: ~0.25 ms per keystroke (≤ 5/s keystroke rate during typing → no UX impact). **Severity**: HIGH (symmetric to F-22). Threat model: an attacker EM-scoping the CPU during the 60-120 s recovery-typing window can collect per-keystroke traces and reconstruct each word. Camera-blocked attacker with EM access is now defended against in BOTH directions (provisioning via F-22/F-24, recovery via F-25). Full §F-25 in tools/sca/README.md. 1837/1837 secure host tests pass; cargo check clean on both stm32u585+dual-se+bhk and mock-se shapes. |
| 2026-05-19 | **F-26 — Groth16 verifier tight FI sweep — NO FORGE** | New `tools/sca/groth16_target/` (thumbv8m crate mirroring `secure/src/zk/groth16.rs::groth16_verify` verbatim) + `fault_sweep_groth16.py` running rainbow `fault_skip` over the BAD-path tail. Setup: real VK from `secure/src/zk/vk_data.rs` + real proof from `test_vectors.rs`, paired with a `pub0`-bit-flipped public-signal vector so the unfaulted run rejects (r0=0). A single fault flipping r0 to 1 would be FORGE_RELEASE — letting an attacker forge ZK clear-sign verification, which the trusted UI uses to gate human-readable Aave/CowSwap action rendering. Auto-bisects BAD-path instruction count to 1K precision (161,346,878 ± 1K). Sweeps `[bad_total-5000, bad_total-900)` at step 25 = 164 positions. **Result: 124 rejects, 0 accepts, 26 crashes, 14 anomalies — no FORGE_RELEASE.** The final `result == Gt::identity()` compare + return path is FI-robust against single instruction-skips in this window. Two harness corrections landed: (1) sweep upper bound respects rainbow's ~800-instr `IndexError: reached end of function before faulting` boundary (sits ~800 instr before the bisected `bad_total`); diagnosed via a throwaway probe walking deltas 100/200/300/.../5000. (2) Fix `NameError: TIGHT_DEPTH` in success-summary print path; same `FULL_LEAD` bound applied to `--full` (overnight) mode. | 2026-05-19 | **F-27 — recovery candidate-pick gate leaks (2 leaks, both FIXED)** | Sweeping `measured_boot.rs` + `tx/display/*` (per user request) confirmed those paths are clean (data they render is public — firmware_hash words, tx amounts/addresses). The same sweep landed on `secure/src/ui/seed_wizard.rs` and turned up two real production leaks in the recovery candidate-pick gate, both on secret typed-prefix input. **Leak A — `prefix_is_exact_word`** (a second `WORDLIST.binary_search_by` that F-25 didn't reach — used inside the candidate-pick gate when a typed prefix is itself an exact wordlist entry AND a prefix of others, e.g. "act" / "actor"). **Leak B — `render_candidate_screen`** stacked two channels on the same secret-derived idx: `WORDLIST[idx].as_bytes()` (F-22-class load) + `d.draw_line(..., ascii_str(&row))` (F-24-stage-C-class non-CT embedded-graphics font expansion that the seed-display path migrated away from in F-24 but the candidate-pick path didn't). **TVLA**: baseline binary search max|t| = 42.905 over 239 samples (peaks at 197/195/171/194/217 = 11 binary-search midpoint loads); CT scan max|t| = 0.000 over 69,662 samples — same shape as F-25 result. **Fix**: two new public CT primitives in `bip39/src/lib.rs` — `word_bytes_at(idx) -> ([u8; 8], u8)` (thin pub wrapper over F-22-era `ct_load_word`, same 2048-entry mask-OR scan + black_box barriers) and `is_exact_wordlist_entry(needle: &[u8]) -> bool` (adds an explicit `entry_len == needle.len()` check — without it a zero-padded oversized needle "act\0\0\0\0\0" would falsely match the 3-byte entry "act" whose WORDLIST_FLAT storage is itself padded to 8 zero bytes; regression test caught this). `seed_wizard::prefix_is_exact_word` collapses to a one-liner calling `is_exact_wordlist_entry`. `seed_wizard::render_candidate_screen` follows the `render_mnemonic_page` template: `word_bytes_at` for the wordlist load + `flush_with_secret_rows` for the framebuffer paint (rows 0 + 3 stay on `draw_line` — title + hint are public text). **Cost**: ~0.25 ms / `prefix_is_exact_word` call + 2× `word_bytes_at` per `render_candidate_screen`; bounded by keystroke cadence (≤5/s long-presses), no UX impact. **Verification**: 1837/1837 secure host tests pass; 6 new tests in `bip39/tests/lookup.rs` lock in the public-API contract (`word_bytes_at` round-trips every wordlist entry; `is_exact_wordlist_entry` accepts every entry, rejects obvious non-words + the zero-padding trap + over-long inputs + ASCII case-sensitivity at the byte level); `make seedwizard-exact-leak` validates baseline-leaks + CT-clean post-fix. **Deferred hygiene**: `bip39::lookup_word_exact` still uses leaky binary search but its sole caller is `Mnemonic::from_words` invoked only from the e2e-test path with fixed TEST_WORDS (no production secret); `measured_boot::render_all_words` still uses `WORDLIST[idx]` but the displayed words derive from firmware_hash which is public by construction. Both flagged for a separate "delete leaky WORDLIST[i] access pattern" hygiene commit so this F-27 diff stays focused on the production-critical fix. Full §F-27 in tools/sca/README.md. |

| 2026-05-19 | §25 Gap 4 — SE050 silicon-locked SW → `UnlockError::PinLocked` (code landed; bench validation deferred) | Closes the corruption/desync stuck-loop case where MCU page-124 has been reset (flash fault, RDP regression mid-cycle, dev-tool intervention) but SE050 UserID is silicon-locked — pre-fix, a fresh PIN attempt hit the `Status(sw)` catch-all → `InternalError` → gateway treated as transient, no wipe → device stuck. Investigation: original work-todo guess "0x6983" was the **A71CL legacy** code (`SE_HAL_Lib.h::A71CL_SE_HAL_ERR_KEY_LOCKED = 0xCC // 6983`); SE050's `se05x_tlv.h::smStatus_t` does NOT include 0x6983. SE050 uses **0x6986** (`SM_ERR_COMMAND_NOT_ALLOWED`, AN13030 wording "Command not allowed — access denied based on object policy") for policy-denial when an auth object stops accepting VERIFY — the documented-by-deduction match (AN12413 is behind NXP login, on-silicon SW capture deferred). Fix: new `Se050Error::AuthMethodBlocked` variant in `apdu.rs`; `apdu::verify_session` adds `Status(0x6986) → AuthMethodBlocked` arm alongside the existing `0x6985 || 0x63xx → PinIncorrect`; new pure helper `classify_se050_unlock_error(&Se050Error) -> UnlockError` in `mod.rs` maps `AuthMethodBlocked → PinLocked`; `Se050::unlock` map_err refactored to call the helper (side-effect arm zeros `self.remaining` on AuthMethodBlocked). End-to-end dispatch: `apdu::verify_session` → `AuthMethodBlocked` → `Se050::unlock` map_err → `UnlockError::PinLocked` → `DualSecureElement::unlock` propagation → `cmd_request_unlock`'s `Err(UnlockError::PinLocked) => trigger_lockout_wipe()` arm. 4 new host pin-tests `gap4_*` in `se050_under_test/pure_tests.rs` (1841 total, was 1837): (a) apdu arm exists; (b) classifier maps AuthMethodBlocked→PinLocked; (c) PinIncorrect still maps to PinIncorrect (catastrophic-UX regression guard); (d) catch-all still on InternalError (no over-eager wipe on transport glitches). Conservatively narrow per advisor review — only 0x6986 mapped, not 0x6982 (generic, false-positive wipe risk worse than the rare desync). On-silicon validation: next bench session, run `iterative_wipe` against a throwaway UserID to drive it to attempts=0, send VERIFY, capture SW — if not 0x6986 update both the `verify_session` arm and the `AuthMethodBlocked` doc. `pin-gate-wipe-e2e` infrastructure already covers the wiring; just needs SW-capture on the post-lockout attempt. |

| 2026-05-19 | F-24 stage E sub-channel 4 — **Phase 1 (decoy frames) LANDED**; bench flicker validation pending | F-24 stages A-D (2026-05-18) closed the CPU-side leak surface for seed display. The four hardware-only residuals were documented but not implemented. Phase 1 closes sub-channel 4 (I²C bus-EM emission) via persistence-of-vision decoy frames. Implementation: `secure/src/ui/seed_wizard.rs::show_mnemonic` generates N=4 valid BIP-39 mnemonics from independent `rng_strong::fill` entropy at wizard entry. New `show_mnemonic_page_with_decoys` frame loop interleaves the real mnemonic (200 ms hold) with one decoy per cycle (40 ms hold), cycling through all 4 decoys round-robin — 5:1 real:decoy time ratio. Every frame (real OR decoy) routes through the same constant-time `render_mnemonic_page` so the per-frame CPU trace is content-independent (F-24 stages A-D contract preserved). Button events return immediately from the frame loop; idle timeout triggers wipe via the existing path. **Mnemonic's Drop zeroizes indices** so decoys wipe automatically on wizard exit; the raw 32-byte entropy buffers are explicitly zeroized after Mnemonic construction. **Failure path**: if rng_strong::fill fails for any decoy (extremely rare — 3-source XOR'd TRNG would need multiple simultaneous faults), show_mnemonic returns Cancelled with "RNG failed retry" status; wizard outer loop retries. 5 new source-text regression sentinels in `secure/src/ui_under_test/pure_tests.rs::negative_*decoy*` pin: (a) rng_strong as entropy source (not deterministic), (b) Mnemonic::from_entropy for valid BIP-39 checksums (un-filterable from real), (c) entropy zeroize post-construct, (d) 5:1 time ratio constants pinned, (e) frame loop routes through render_mnemonic_page (CT path). 1847/1847 secure host tests pass (was 1842; +5 sentinels). cargo check clean on stm32u585+dual-se+bhk+ui-oled with and without debug-log. **Hardware flicker validation outstanding** — bench user reads 24 words off real silicon (SSD1306 @ 400 kHz I²C, ~36 ms per flush_with_secret_rows call) and reports whether the 200ms:40ms cadence is readable. If unreadable, increase REAL_FRAME_HOLD_MS to 400-500 (smooths visual, weakens defense). **Phase 2 deferred**: constant-pixel-count encoding (sub-channel 3, power-rail) requires per-word XOR-complement tables that bring every row's lit-pixel count to a constant — significantly more work than Phase 1; revisit after Phase 1's UX is validated. Threat-model boundary: Phase 1 defends an EM-bus attacker without optical access (foil-wrapped device, dark room, internal RMA scenarios). The realistic threat (camera attacker) is unaffected — decoys are still visible to the camera; the user's responsibility for physical privacy during provisioning is unchanged. tools/sca/README.md §F-24 sub-channel 4 updated to reflect Phase 1 landing + Phase 2 deferred. |
| 2026-05-19 | F-24 stage E Phase 1 — bench-tested on OLED, defense doesn't work on bistable displays; code gated behind `decoy-frames` feature pending LCD hardware | New `decoy-flicker-test` Cargo feature + `make decoy-flicker-hw` Makefile target. Short-circuits `main()` into a forever-loop that renders page 0 of a fixed test mnemonic interleaved with 4 fixed decoys at the production cadence — no wizard, no buttons, no SE access, just OLED rendering for bench visual inspection. Result: at 5:1 (200ms:40ms) the user sees N+1 mnemonics cycling, NOT a stable real with subliminal decoys. Bumping REAL_FRAME_HOLD_MS to 2000 didn't help (user still perceives content changes, not flicker). **Root cause**: OLED pixels are bistable — once painted, they hold state indefinitely until repainted. Persistence-of-vision (Trezor's referenced mechanism) works on CRTs / movie projectors (natural decay between frames); on OLEDs the eye locks onto every painted frame. We cannot paint faster than ~25 ms over I²C @ 400 kHz, and a 25 ms content change is well above the eye's flicker-fusion threshold (~10 ms for content). **Code disposition**: Phase 1 decoy-frame code stays in tree (`show_mnemonic_with_decoys`, `show_mnemonic_page_with_decoys`) gated behind a new `decoy-frames` Cargo feature (default OFF). `show_mnemonic` now splits to `show_mnemonic_simple` (default) vs `show_mnemonic_with_decoys` (feature-gated). 5 source-text regression sentinels preserved. Production wizard reverts to the simple stable-render path so users can read their seed. `make decoy-flicker-hw` target stays for future revalidation. **Path forward**: re-test on the ZT165M017AT TFT LCD (NV3007 driver, SPI, 142×428, Tr+Tf typ 35 ms max 40 ms) when it's wired. On slow-response LCDs the pixel response time IS the persistence-of-vision mechanism — a decoy painted for ~5-10 ms and immediately overwritten by real never reaches full pixel transition. Tune REAL_FRAME_HOLD_MS / DECOY_FRAME_HOLD_MS empirically on the LCD via `decoy-flicker-test`, then flip `decoy-frames` ON in the shipping profile if validated. Phase 2 (constant-pixel-count encoding for sub-channel 3 power-rail) also becomes less necessary on the LCD: backlight current is constant regardless of pixel state (3 white LEDs in parallel, ~60 mA typ), so the power-rail leak signature on the LCD is structurally weaker than OLED. tools/sca/README.md §F-24 sub-channel 4 updated with full bench finding + LCD path. 1847/1847 host tests pass (5 regression sentinels still match the in-tree decoy code). |

| 2026-05-19 | Factory provisioning — Phase A.1: rehearsal mode + compile fence + OTP sentinel + RDP2 sequencing docs | Three foot-gun protections added on top of the Phase A scaffold (commit `cab0530`): **(1) Rehearsal mode** via new `factory-provisioning-rehearsal` Cargo feature — steps 4 (DualSeProvision), 5 (WipeUserState), 6 (PostWipeValidation) SKIP their destructive calls in this build. OLED still cycles all 7 panels for visual validation; OTP sentinel still gets written but with `BIT_REHEARSAL` (not `BIT_PRODUCTION`). Distinct success panel "REHEARSAL OK / SE NOT changed / NOT for ship!" — operator + host fixture can't confuse it for production. New `make build-hw-factory-provisioning-rehearsal` target. **(2) Compile-time fence** — refuses to build the irreversible production profile (`optiga-lock-operational` or `bhk` without hardcoded-master-key or NEITHER `dev-testkey` NOR `otp-hardcoded-master-key`) without an explicit `factory-production-irreversible-im-sure` opt-in feature. Catches the "I forgot dev-testkey in the Makefile" class of accidental chip burns. Anyone editing Cargo can add/remove the opt-in — this is a foot-gun guard, not a security gate. Empirically validated: all 5 build paths (default, factory+dev-testkey, factory+rehearsal, factory+LcsO=Op+opt-in, factory+LcsO=Op-without-opt-in→compile error). **(3) OTP factory sentinel** at byte offset 160 of the STM32 OTP region (one 128-bit quad-word, just past the device master key). 3-bit tally encoding: bit 0 = "ran at least once", bit 1 = "rehearsal completed", bit 2 = "production completed". One-way per OTP semantics, but rehearsal-then-production is the expected dev iteration path (results in `0xFFFFFFF8` = both bits cleared). The host-side factory fixture reads the sentinel via probe-rs at `0x0BFA_00A0` BEFORE bumping the irreversible RDP=Level-2 option byte; fixture interprets `0xFFFFFFFA` (production only) or `0xFFFFFFF8` (both modes) as RDP2-ready, refuses RDP2 on any other state. Step 7 (new) writes the sentinel; the pre-populated-state check (step 3) also checks the sentinel + refuses to re-run a chip whose `BIT_PRODUCTION` is already cleared (`E0702`). New OTP API: `factory_sentinel_read() -> u32` + `unsafe factory_sentinel_record(bits_to_clear) -> Result<(), OtpError>`. `OTP_RESERVED_BYTES` bumped from 160 to 176 to include the sentinel region; matching host test updated. **Operator manual** (`docs/factory-provisioning.md`) updated with: rehearsal-mode panel preview + interpretation table, OTP sentinel encoding + host-fixture decode table, RDP2 section explaining the post-ceremony irreversible step + why the fixture must verify the sentinel before bumping, build-profile safety guard table. 1847/1847 standard host tests pass; 7 factory_provisioning tests (was 5 — added step-count + factory_sentinel_bit_layout). cargo check clean on all 5 build paths (4 valid + 1 deliberately-refused-by-fence). |
| 2026-05-19 | Factory provisioning — Phase A.2: flash + verify + RDP2-bump Makefile targets, early "ran" sentinel | Wires the host-side orchestration on top of Phase A.1's foot-gun protections. **Three new Makefile targets** + 1 helper script: (a) `make flash-hw-factory-provisioning` chains probe-rs download (NS + S) + STM32_Programmer_CLI TZ option-byte config + probe-rs reset + the new verifier script. Does NOT bump RDP2. (b) `make flash-hw-factory-provisioning-rehearsal` same as above but uses the rehearsal build (steps 4-6 skip destructive calls). (c) `make bump-rdp2-after-factory` re-reads the OTP sentinel, refuses unless `BIT_PRODUCTION` is cleared, prompts the operator to type `BUMP RDP2` as final confirmation, then runs `STM32_Programmer_CLI --optionbytes RDP=0xCC`. (d) `make factory-status-hw` read-only sentinel inspection (no flashing, no state change). **Host-side verifier script** `tools/factory-provisioning-verify.sh`: polls the OTP factory sentinel at `0x0BFA_00A0` via `probe-rs read 32` for up to 60s, requires the sentinel value to be stable across two successive reads (guards against mid-write race), decodes into one of 5 outcomes (DID_NOT_START / STARTED_FAILED / REHEARSAL_ONLY / PRODUCTION_OK / BOTH_OK), maps each to a script exit code (0=RDP2-eligible, 1=not-yet-complete, 2=probe failure, 3=corrupt sentinel, 4=bump refused). `--bump-rdp2` flag does the RDP2 transition only if the sentinel is RDP2-eligible AND the operator types `BUMP RDP2` at the interactive prompt. Configurable via `CHIP` / `OTP_SENTINEL_ADDR` / `POLL_TIMEOUT_SECS` / `POLL_INTERVAL_SECS` env vars. **Firmware fix**: `factory_provisioning::run_and_halt` now writes `BIT_RAN` to the OTP sentinel BEFORE step 1 (was only at step 7). This lets the host verifier distinguish "chip never started" (`0xFFFFFFFF`) from "started + halted at failure panel" (`0xFFFFFFFE`) — without the early write, both states looked identical and the host fixture would timeout on every failure. The early write is best-effort (silent on OTP fault) so a stuck OTP write doesn't brick the ceremony at entry. **Operator manual** updated: new "Quick procedure" section showing the three-make-target flow, new "Source map" entries for the script + targets. All 5 cargo build paths still clean; 1847 standard host tests pass; shell-script `bash -n` syntax check clean. Target NOT silicon-validated by intent — phase B awaits the user's hardware-on-bench session. |

| 2026-05-19 | Prodtest firmware — Phase C (communication tests: OPTIGA + SE050 + USB integrity) | Adds the third tier of prodtest commands — communication tests that exercise the I²C and USB stacks end-to-end. **3 new commands at IDs 106-108:** `CMD_PRODTEST_OPTIGA_HANDSHAKE` lazily runs `OptigaTrustM::init()` (RST pulse + `OpenApplication`) then `GetRandom(16)`. On a fresh chip there's no PBS yet so the APDU goes through the plain non-shielded path (`shield.active == false` → `send_command` falls through to `transceive` without wrapping). Catches missing chip / broken solder / wedged I²C / wrong RST line / power-rail or clock issues / chip RNG defect (all-zero or all-0xFF). `CMD_PRODTEST_SE050_HANDSHAKE` same shape — `Se050::init()` (interface_reset + ATR + SCP03 session with NXP default platform keys) then `GetRandom(16)`. Catches missing chip / wrong ENA line / cold-boot timing (the existing 3-attempt retry loop already covers this) / pre-rotated SCP03 keys (chip wasn't blank) / RNG defect. `CMD_PRODTEST_USB_LOOPBACK` echoes N≤256 bytes — the fact the firmware received the command already proves USB RX works; this proves TX + full round-trip byte integrity for non-trivial payloads. Catches USB OTG FS TX corruption / HID report fragmentation bugs in the NS-side stack / buffer-overflow in the transport layer / sustained-TX VCC instability. **Wiring:** 3 new `#[cfg(feature = "prodtest")]` dispatch arms in `secure/src/nsc/mod.rs`, 3 new CMSE veneers (`nsc_prodtest_optiga_handshake` / `nsc_prodtest_se050_handshake` / `nsc_prodtest_usb_loopback`), 3 new handlers in `secure/src/nsc/prodtest.rs` that access the global `crate::SE` via the established `addr_of_mut!(crate::SE)` pattern (serialised by the dispatcher's non-reentrant invariant). The handshake handlers reuse `OptigaTrustM::random` / `Se050::random` so the test surface is just "does the existing random primitive work" — no new code path to bench-validate. **Host fixture runner** (`tools/factory-prodtest-runner.py`) gets `test_optiga_handshake` + `test_se050_handshake` (both check status==Ok + 16 bytes received + bytes ≠ all-zero / all-0xFF, log RNG output for per-die-uniqueness DB) + `test_usb_loopback` (deterministic XOR-with-0xA5 pattern catches byte-substitution + bit-flip + buffer-overflow, reports first-mismatch offset for diagnostics). New module pin-tests: `positive_phase_c_handshake_rng_lens_pinned` + `positive_usb_loopback_cap_matches_proto_doc`. Operator manual (`docs/factory-prodtest.md`) command table extended to 9 commands; per-command sections added with pass criteria + diagnostic value for each. **Build validation:** 4 build paths still pass — default (no prodtest), prodtest+dev-testkey, prodtest+factory-production-irreversible-im-sure, prodtest-without-opt-in→compile error (fence still fires). 1847/1847 default-features host tests pass; module-internal tests still pending host harness wiring (same situation as Phase A+B + LCD + factory_provisioning modules). `make build-hw-prodtest` succeeds. Python runner `--help` works; syntax check clean. Target NOT silicon-validated by intent — Phase B awaits user's hardware-on-bench session. |
| 2026-05-19 | Prodtest firmware — Phase D (interactive button test) | `CMD_PRODTEST_BUTTON_TEST` (109) — 3-step LEFT/RIGHT/BOTH verification, 10 s per step, returns 4 bytes (step_status:1 + reserved:3). Compact step-status encoding: upper nibble = step (1/2/3), lower nibble = error kind (1=timeout, 2=wrong button = swapped wires). Wrong-button detection (advisor): if RIGHT fires during the LEFT step or vice versa, return WRONG_BUTTON instead of TIMEOUT — operator + factory error table get a diagnostically-distinct path (swapped wires = rewire + retry; dead solder = re-solder + retry). No latency reporting — human reaction time dominates GPIO; the press-registered-or-not signal carries all the information. **Wiring:** `gpio-buttons` added to the `prodtest = [...]` Cargo feature; `hw::buttons::{left_pressed, right_pressed, busy_wait_ms}` promoted from private to `pub(crate)` so the prodtest handler can run its own poll loop without going through `wait_event`'s gesture state machine; `hw::buttons::init()` invocation added to the prodtest short-circuit in `secure/src/main.rs` (gated on `gpio-buttons` so the call is no-op on non-prodtest builds that also enable the feature). Handler runs the 3-step state machine with 30 ms debounce + 5 ms poll + clean-release-before-next-step (so a held button doesn't bleed into the next prompt). OLED shows "PRESS LEFT" / "PRESS RIGHT" / "PRESS BOTH" with 10 s reminder, then "BTN PASS" / "BTN FAIL step N". **Host runner** (`tools/factory-prodtest-runner.py`) gets `test_button_test` + `BUTTON_STEP_DECODE` table that maps each status code (0x00 / 0x11 / 0x12 / 0x21 / 0x22 / 0x31) to operator-readable diagnostic — `step 1 (LEFT) wrong button — operator pressed RIGHT or wires are swapped`. Added LAST in the test orchestration since it's the only operator-interactive command (failures from automated tests are easier to recover from without involving a human). **3 new pin-tests** pin the step-code layout (compact nibble encoding invariant), the 10 s timeout, and the 4-byte output length. Operator manual gets a full per-step-status decode table + a `<!-- TODO photo: ... -->` marker (advisor: leave honest markers rather than describing photos with prose that will age badly). **Pre-Phase-E blocker surfaced during this work**: the `prodtest` main-loop short-circuit halts in WFI before reaching `boot_ns::boot`, so the NS USB stack never starts and no prodtest command is reachable over USB yet. The button test code is correct in isolation; bench validation requires Phase E to first fix the NS-launch flow. Tracked in work-todo §30 status line. 4 build paths still clean (default, prodtest+dev-testkey, prodtest+factory-production-irreversible-im-sure, prodtest-no-opt-in→compile error). 1847/1847 default-features host tests pass. Python `--help` works; AST clean. Target NOT silicon-validated by intent — awaits Phase E + user's hardware-on-bench session. |
| 2026-05-19 | Prodtest firmware — Phase E (USB launch fix + INS dispatch + Python HID framing) | First Phase that makes the prodtest commands actually reachable over USB. **Three pieces:** (1) **Firmware USB-launch fix**: the prodtest main-loop short-circuit was halting in WFI before `boot_ns::boot`, so the NS USB stack never started — every "Phase A/B/C/D done" was firmware-only and not actually reachable from a host. Rewired to run the final-stage init that NS world depends on (`hw::usb_hw::init`, `setup_systick`, DWT cycle counter) then call `boot_ns::boot(NS_FLASH_BASE)` — same exit primitive the production main flow uses. The "PRODTEST READY" OLED panel shows briefly then NS world takes over. (2) **Per-CMD INS allocation + NS dispatch**: 10 new `INS_V2_PRODTEST_*` constants at 0x80..0x89 in `proto/src/lib.rs` (one INS per command — no cmd_id overhead inside the APDU body, full LC=255 budget per command). Mechanical mapping `INS = 0x80 + (CMD - 100)`. NS-side: 10 extern `nsc_prodtest_*` declarations in `nonsecure/src/nsc_api.rs::transport`, 10 `pub(super)` transport wrappers, 10 `pub fn prodtest_*` public API entries. NS USB dispatcher (`nonsecure/src/usb/commands.rs`): 10 `#[cfg(feature = "prodtest")]` match arms + 10 `cmd_prodtest_*` methods + new `prodtest_finalize(n, status)` helper that writes outputs to `RESP_BUF[..n]` followed by 2-byte SW (SW_OK=0x9000 on Ok, SW_INTERNAL_ERROR=0x6F00 otherwise). USB_LOOPBACK capped at 254 B (255 - 2 B SW headroom inside the 256 B `RESP_BUF`). Nonsecure Cargo gets a new `prodtest = ["stm32u585", "usb"]` feature. Makefile `build-hw-prodtest` updated to pass the new feature to nonsecure crate. (3) **Python host-side HID framing**: `tools/factory-prodtest-runner.py::ProdtestTransport` rewritten from a stub into the production transport — `_build_apdu` (CLA=0xF0 + INS + P1=0 + P2=0 + LC + data; LC ≤ 255 verified pre-build), `_frame_apdu` (chan/tag/seq/total_len header, 57 B first chunk + 59 B continuation chunks, byte-for-byte mirror of `tools/webhid_test.html::frameApdu`), `_read_response` (state machine matching `shared/src/apdu_framing.rs::HidFrameAssembler`'s expected/seq/chan_id reassembly). Linux hidapi quirk handled (advisor): leading 0x00 report-ID byte on `device.write` (kernel hidraw inspects byte 0 as report ID even when the descriptor has none); `device.read` does NOT include the byte so reassembly reads 64-byte frames directly. Status code mapping: SW_OK → STATUS_OK = 0 (so existing test_* wrappers' `status != STATUS_OK` checks Just Work); other SW values pass through unchanged so error logs preserve the wire SW. 60 s read timeout to accommodate BUTTON_TEST's ~30 s budget. INS_FOR_CMD lookup table for cmd→INS translation. **Build validation**: 4 secure-side build paths still clean (default, prodtest+dev-testkey, prodtest+factory-production-irreversible-im-sure, prodtest-no-opt-in→fence fires). New `make build-hw-prodtest` builds both secure + nonsecure cleanly with `--features stm32u585,usb,prodtest` on the NS side. 1847/1847 default-features host tests pass. Python AST clean; `--help` works. Operator manual extended with "Phase E wire format" section documenting APDU + HID framing layout + Linux hidapi quirk. Target NOT silicon-validated by intent — Phase B awaits the user's hardware-on-bench session, which can now actually drive every prodtest CMD over USB rather than being firmware-only. |
| 2026-05-19 | Prodtest firmware — Phase F (operator manual text; photos pending hardware) | Operator-facing documentation polish. Three new sections in `docs/factory-prodtest.md`: (1) **Pre-flight checklist** — 5 items the operator runs once at shift start before any units are tested: fixture USB cable golden-unit verify (catches station-side issues vs. unit-under-test), probe-rs flash speed sanity (> 30 s indicates adapter/hub problem), DISPLAY_PATTERN visual-distinguishability under ambient lighting, anti-static wristband continuity check, defective-unit bin labeled (NEVER back on the line). (2) **Troubleshooting matrix** — every per-command failure mode mapped to a likely root cause + operator action: GET_ID UID==0/0xFF → REPORT VENDOR (silicon dead); OPTIGA/SE050 timeout → reseat shield, retry; HANDSHAKE rng==0/0xFF → I²C bus pulled high/low (rewire); USB_LOOPBACK byte-mismatch → if one unit: set aside; if multiple: REPORT VENDOR (likely firmware); BUTTON_TEST 0x12/0x22 → swapped wires (rewire); 0x11/0x21 → re-solder button; 0x31 → operator demo. "REPORT VENDOR" means tag + photograph + send chip UID + lot number to firmware team; don't attempt board-level repair on silicon-defect chips because repair time exceeds BOM. (3) **TODO photo markers** at every visual-aid moment in the manual (`<!-- TODO photo: side-by-side OLED snapshots of each of the 5 patterns ... -->`, `<!-- TODO photo: per-status decision tree as a printable wallchart ... -->`, etc.). Per advisor: markers describe what the photo SHOULD show; they don't paraphrase the visual into prose because (a) the operator's authority is the chip in front of them, not the manual, and (b) descriptions of UI states age into wrong-but-shippable documentation the moment the firmware changes a glyph. Photos land in a follow-up when the user runs a hardware-on-bench session with a USB-C camera. Phase F roadmap entry updated in the operator manual + work-todo: text DONE, photos blocked on bench. No firmware changes. 1847/1847 default-features host tests still pass (text-only change, no impact). |
